📚 NCERT Class 10 Maths, Ex 9.1
Q51. If sec ⁡ + tan ⁡ = 2 secθ+tanθ=2, then sin ⁡ sinθ equals:
A 3/5
B 4/5
C 5/13
D 12/13
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (a)
💡 Given sec ⁡ + tan ⁡ = 2 secθ+tanθ=2. Also, sec ⁡ − tan ⁡ = 1 sec ⁡ + tan ⁡ = 1 2 secθ−tanθ= secθ+tanθ 1 = 2 1 . Adding: 2 sec ⁡ = 2 + 1 2 = 5 2 ⇒ sec ⁡ = 5 4 ⇒ cos ⁡ = 4 5 2secθ=2+ 2 1 = 2 5 ⇒secθ= 4 5 ⇒cosθ= 5 4 . Subtracting: 2 tan ⁡ = 2 − 1 2 = 3 2 ⇒ tan ⁡ = 3 4 ⇒ sin ⁡ = tan ⁡ ⋅ cos ⁡ = 3 4 × 4 5 = 3 5 2tanθ=2− 2 1 = 2 3 ⇒tanθ= 4 3 ⇒sinθ=tanθ⋅cosθ= 4 3 × 5 4 = 5 3 .

💡 tan ⁡ + cot ⁡ = sin ⁡ cos ⁡ + cos ⁡ sin ⁡ = sin ⁡ 2 + cos ⁡ 2 sin ⁡ cos ⁡ = 1 sin ⁡ cos ⁡ tanθ+cotθ= cosθ sinθ + sinθ cosθ = sinθcosθ sin 2 θ+cos 2 θ = sinθcosθ 1 . Given 1 sin ⁡ cos ⁡ = 2 ⇒ sin ⁡ cos ⁡ = 1 2 sinθcosθ 1 =2⇒sinθcosθ= 2 1 .

Q53. In a right triangle, the hypotenuse is 10 cm and one acute angle is 30°. The length of the side opposite to this angle is:
A 5 5 cm
B 5^3 cm
C 10^3 cm
D 10 √3 10 cm
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (a)
💡 sin ⁡ 30 ∘ = opposite hypotenuse ⇒ 1 2 = opp 10 ⇒ opp = 5 sin30 ∘ = hypotenuse opposite ⇒ 2 1 = 10 opp ⇒opp=5 cm.

Q54. The value of sin ⁡ 2 5 ∘ + sin ⁡ 2 10 ∘ + sin ⁡ 2 15 ∘ + ⋯ + sin ⁡ 2 85 ∘ sin 2 5 ∘ +sin 2 10 ∘ +sin 2 15 ∘ +⋯+sin 2 85 ∘ is:
A 8 8
B 8.5 8.5
C 9 9
D 9.5 9.5
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (b)
💡 There are 17 terms (5,10,...,85). Pair them as ( sin ⁡ 2 5 ∘ + sin ⁡ 2 85 ∘ ) = sin ⁡ 2 5 ∘ + cos ⁡ 2 5 ∘ = 1 (sin 2 5 ∘ +sin 2 85 ∘ )=sin 2 5 ∘ +cos 2 5 ∘ =1, similarly up to ( sin ⁡ 2 40 ∘ + sin ⁡ 2 50 ∘ ) = 1 (sin 2 40 ∘ +sin 2 50 ∘ )=1. That's 8 pairs sum to 1 each, total 8. The middle term sin ⁡ 2 45 ∘ = 1 2 sin 2 45 ∘ = 2 1 . Sum = 8 + 0.5 = 8.5 8+0.5=8.5.

B 20 ∘ 20 ∘
C 30 ∘ 30 ∘

B 8 √3 8 m
C 16 16 m
D 16 √3 16 m

Better: the standing part is the remaining vertical stump. From the triangle, tan ⁡ 30 ∘ = ℎ 8 ⇒ ℎ = 8 3 tan30 ∘ = 8 h ⇒h= 3 8 . Total height = ℎ + = 8 3 + 16 3 = 24 3 = 8 3 h+L= 3 8 + 3 16 = 3 24 =8 3 m.

A 1 1
B 2 2
C 3 3
D 4 4

B 25 25 m
C 50^3 m
D 50 50 m
Show Solution

After walking 50 m, new distance = − 50 d−50, tan ⁡ 60 ∘ = ℎ − 50 ⇒ 3 = ℎ ℎ 3 − 50 tan60 ∘ = d−50 h ⇒ 3 = h 3 −50 h . 3 ( ℎ 3 − 50 ) = ℎ ⇒ 3 ℎ − 50 3 = ℎ ⇒ 2 ℎ = 50 3 ⇒ ℎ = 25 √3 (h 3 −50)=h⇒3h−50 3 =h⇒2h=50 3 ⇒h=25 3 m.

Q84. If sec ⁡ = + 1 4 secθ=x+ 4x 1 , then sec ⁡ + tan ⁡ secθ+tanθ is:

✅ Answer: (a)
💡 cos ⁡ 2 = 1 − 2 2 + 2 = 2 2 + 2 cos 2 θ=1− a 2 +b 2 a 2 = a 2 +b 2 b 2 . For acute angle, cos ⁡ = 2 + 2 cosθ= a 2 +b 2 b .

Q88. If sin ⁡ = 3 5 sinθ= 5 3 , then cosec ⁡ − cot ⁡ cosecθ−cotθ equals:
A 1/3
B 3/5
C 5/3
D 4/3
Show Solution

📚 NCERT Class 10 Maths
Q89. A man on the top of a light house observes a ship coming towards it. If the angle of depression changes from 30° to 60° in 2 minutes, the time taken by the ship to reach the light house from the second position is:
A 1 1 min
B 2 2 min
C 3 3 min
D 4 4 min
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (a) 1 min
💡 Let height = h. Position 1 (30°): distance = h cot30° = h√3. Position 2 (60°): distance = h cot60° = h/√3. Distance covered = h√3 - h/√3 = h*( (3-1)/√3 ) = 2h/√3. Speed = distance/time = (2h/√3)/2 = h/√3 per min. Remaining distance to lighthouse from 2nd position = h/√3. Time = (h/√3) / (h/√3) = 1 min.
📚 Competitive exam (height & distance)
Q90. If sin ⁡ + cos ⁡ = 2 sin ⁡ ( 90 ∘ − ) sinθ+cosθ= 2 sin(90 ∘ −θ), then tan ⁡ tanθ is equal to:
A 2 − 1 2 −1
B 2 + 1 2 +1
C 1 1
D 2 − 2 2− 2
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (a)
💡 RHS: sin ⁡ ( 90 ∘ − ) = cos ⁡ sin(90 ∘ −θ)=cosθ. So equation: sin ⁡ + cos ⁡ = 2 cos ⁡ ⇒ sin ⁡ = ( 2 − 1 ) cos ⁡ ⇒ tan ⁡ = 2 − 1 sinθ+cosθ= 2 cosθ⇒sinθ=( 2 −1)cosθ⇒tanθ= 2 −1.

Q91. 1 sec ⁡ − tan ⁡ − 1 cos ⁡ secθ−tanθ 1 − cosθ 1 is equal to:
A 0 0
B 1 1
C sec ⁡ secθ
D tan ⁡ tanθ
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (d)

Q95. If tan ⁡ + sec ⁡ = 3 tanθ+secθ=3, then sin ⁡ sinθ equals:
A 4/5
B 3/5
C 5/13
D 12/13
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (a)

Adding: 2 sec ⁡ = 3 + 1 3 = 10 3 ⇒ sec ⁡ = 5 3 ⇒ cos ⁡ = 3 5 2secθ=3+ 3 1 = 3 10 ⇒secθ= 3 5 ⇒cosθ= 5 3 . Then sin ⁡ = tan ⁡ ⋅ cos ⁡ = 4 3 ⋅ 3 5 = 4 5 sinθ=tanθ⋅cosθ= 3 4 ⋅ 5 3 = 5 4 .

A 44 44

A 1/6
B 1/3
C 1/4

Q98. The shadow of a tower standing on level ground is found to be 45 m longer when the sun's altitude is 30° than when it was 60°. The height of the tower is:
A 45^3 m
B 45 3 2 2 45 3 m
C 45 ( 3 + 1 ) 45( 3 +1) m
D 45 ( 3 − 1 ) 45( 3 −1) m
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (b)
💡 Let height = h. Shadow at 60° = h cot60° = h/√3. Shadow at 30° = h cot30° = h√3. Difference: h√3 - h/√3 = 45 => h( (3-1)/√3 ) = 45 => h(2/√3)=45 => h = 45√3/2.

Show Solution

Q109. If sin ⁡ + cos ⁡ = 2 cos ⁡ sinθ+cosθ= 2 cosθ, then cos ⁡ − sin ⁡ cosθ−sinθ is:
A 2 cos ⁡ 2 cosθ
B 2 sin ⁡ 2 sinθ
C cos ⁡ cosθ
D sin ⁡ sinθ
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (b)

Q111. A pole of height ℎ h stands on the ground. From a point on the ground the angle of elevation of the top of the pole is α and after walking a distance d towards the pole it becomes β. Which of the following gives the height ℎ h?
A cot ⁡ − cot ⁡ cotα−cotβ d
B tan ⁡ − tan ⁡ tanβ−tanα d
C tan ⁡ tan ⁡ tan ⁡ − tan ⁡ tanβ−tanα dtanαtanβ
D tan ⁡ + tan ⁡ tanα+tanβ d
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (c)

Solve for ℎ h: ℎ = tan ⁡ tan ⁡ tan ⁡ − tan ⁡ h= tanβ−tanα dtanαtanβ .

Q118. If tan ⁡ + cot ⁡ = 2 tanθ+cotθ=2, then tan ⁡ 100 + cot ⁡ 100 tan 100 θ+cot 100 θ is:
A 0 0
B 1 1
C 2 2
D 2^100
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (c)
💡 tan ⁡ + cot ⁡ = 2 ⇒ tan ⁡ + 1 / tan ⁡ = 2 ⇒ tan ⁡ 2 − 2 tan ⁡ + 1 = 0 ⇒ ( tan ⁡ − 1 ) 2 = 0 ⇒ tan ⁡ = 1 tanθ+cotθ=2⇒tanθ+1/tanθ=2⇒tan 2 θ−2tanθ+1=0⇒(tanθ−1) 2 =0⇒tanθ=1. So cot ⁡ = 1 cotθ=1. Then tan ⁡ 100 + cot ⁡ 100 = 1 + 1 = 2 tan 100 θ+cot 100 θ=1+1=2.
📚 Competitive exam (trick)
Q119. 1 − tan ⁡ 2 30 ∘ 1 + tan ⁡ 2 30 ∘ 1+tan 2 30 ∘ 1−tan 2 30 ∘ simplifies to:
A cos ⁡ 60 ∘ cos60 ∘
B sin ⁡ 30 ∘ sin30 ∘
C tan ⁡ 60 ∘ tan60 ∘
D cot ⁡ 60 ∘ cot60 ∘
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (a)
💡 tan ⁡ 30 ∘ = 1 / 3 tan30 ∘ =1/ 3 . Expression = 1 − 1 / 3 1 + 1 / 3 = 2 / 3 4 / 3 = 1 2 1+1/3 1−1/3 = 4/3 2/3 = 2 1 . cos ⁡ 60 ∘ = 1 / 2 cos60 ∘ =1/2.

D a c

Q121. If = sin ⁡ x=asinθ and = tan ⁡ y=btanθ, then 2 2 − 2 2 x 2 a 2 − y 2 b 2 is equal to:
A 0 0
B 1 1
C 2 2 b 2 a 2
D 2 2 a 2 b 2
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (b)
💡 sin ⁡ = / sinθ=x/a, tan ⁡ = / tanθ=y/b. We have sec ⁡ 2 = 1 + tan ⁡ 2 sec 2 θ=1+tan 2 θ and cosec ⁡ 2 = 1 + cot ⁡ 2 cosec 2 θ=1+cot 2 θ. But here, cosec ⁡ = / cosecθ=a/x, cot ⁡ = / cotθ=b/y. Using identity cosec ⁡ 2 − cot ⁡ 2 = 1 cosec 2 θ−cot 2 θ=1, we get 2 2 − 2 2 = 1 x 2 a 2 − y 2 b 2 =1.
📚 Competitive exam (Hard)
Q122. The angle of elevation of a cloud from a point 60 m above a lake is 30° and the angle of depression of its reflection in the lake is 60°. The height of the cloud above the lake is:
A 60 60 m
B 120 120 m
C 90 90 m
D 80 80 m
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (b)
💡 Let height of cloud above lake = ℎ h. Observer is 60 m above lake. So height of cloud above observer = ℎ − 60 h−60. Horizontal distance = d. tan ⁡ 30 ∘ = ℎ − 60 ⇒ 1 3 = ℎ − 60 ⇒ = ( ℎ − 60 ) 3 tan30 ∘ = d h−60 ⇒ 3 1 = d h−60 ⇒d=(h−60) 3 . Reflection appears as deep below water as cloud is above, so distance from observer to reflection = 60 + ℎ 60+h vertically. Angle of depression to reflection = 60°, so tan ⁡ 60 ∘ = 60 + ℎ ⇒ 3 = ℎ + 60 tan60 ∘ = d 60+h ⇒ 3 = d h+60 . Substitute d: 3 = ℎ + 60 ( ℎ − 60 ) 3 ⇒ 3 = ℎ + 60 ℎ − 60 ⇒ 3 ℎ − 180 = ℎ + 60 ⇒ 2 ℎ = 240 ⇒ ℎ = 120 3 = (h−60) 3 h+60 ⇒3= h−60 h+60 ⇒3h−180=h+60⇒2h=240⇒h=120 m.

C mn

So = sin ⁡ 4 cos ⁡ 2 mn= cos 2 θ sin 4 θ , = sin ⁡ 2 cos ⁡ mn = cosθ sin 2 θ . And 4 tan ⁡ sin ⁡ = 4 sin ⁡ cos ⁡ sin ⁡ = 4 sin ⁡ 2 cos ⁡ = 4 4tanθsinθ=4 cosθ sinθ sinθ=4 cosθ sin 2 θ =4 mn .

Q125. The shadow of a tower on a level ground increases by 30 m when the sun's altitude changes from 60° to 30°. The height of the tower is:
A 15^3 m
B 30 30 m
C 30^3 m
D 15 15 m
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (a)
💡 Let height = ℎ h. When altitude 60°, shadow = ℎ cot ⁡ 60 ∘ = ℎ / 3 hcot60 ∘ =h/ 3 . When altitude 30°, shadow = ℎ cot ⁡ 30 ∘ = ℎ 3 hcot30 ∘ =h 3 . Difference = ℎ 3 − ℎ / 3 = ℎ ( 3 − 1 3 ) = 2 ℎ 3 = 30 h 3 −h/ 3 =h( 3 3−1 )= 3 2h =30. So ℎ = 30 ⋅ 3 2 = 15 3 h=30⋅ 2 3 =15 3 m.
📚 NCERT Class 10 Maths (Ex 9.1); competitive exam Hard variant
Q126. If cosec ⁡ − cot ⁡ = 2 cosecθ−cotθ= 2 , then cosec ⁡ + cot ⁡ cosecθ+cotθ equals:
A 1 √2 1
B √2
C 2/2
D 1/2
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (a)
💡 We use the identity cosec ⁡ 2 − cot ⁡ 2 = 1 cosec 2 θ−cot 2 θ=1. ⇒ ( cosec ⁡ − cot ⁡ ) ( cosec ⁡ + cot ⁡ ) = 1 ⇒(cosecθ−cotθ)(cosecθ+cotθ)=1 ⇒ 2 × ( cosec ⁡ + cot ⁡ ) = 1 ⇒ cosec ⁡ + cot ⁡ = 1 2 ⇒ 2 ×(cosecθ+cotθ)=1⇒cosecθ+cotθ= 2 1 .

Q128. sin ⁡ 2 5 ∘ + sin ⁡ 2 10 ∘ + sin ⁡ 2 15 ∘ + ⋯ + sin ⁡ 2 90 ∘ sin 2 5 ∘ +sin 2 10 ∘ +sin 2 15 ∘ +⋯+sin 2 90 ∘ equals:

Q131. From the top of a 60 m high lighthouse, the angles of depression of two ships on the same side of the lighthouse are 30° and 45°. The distance between the ships is:
A 60 ( 3 + 1 ) 60( 3 +1) m
B 60 ( 3 − 1 ) 60( 3 −1) m
C 60^3 m
D 60 3 − 60 3 60 −60 m
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (b)
💡 Distance of ship 1 (45°): 1 = 60 cot ⁡ 45 ∘ = 60 d 1 =60cot45 ∘ =60 m. Distance of ship 2 (30°): 2 = 60 cot ⁡ 30 ∘ = 60 3 d 2 =60cot30 ∘ =60 3 m. Distance between ships = 2 − 1 = 60 3 − 60 = 60 ( 3 − 1 ) d 2 −d 1 =60 3 −60=60( 3 −1) m.

Q132. If sin ⁡ + sin ⁡ 2 = 1 sinθ+sin 2 θ=1, then the value of cos ⁡ 12 + 3 cos ⁡ 10 + 3 cos ⁡ 8 + cos ⁡ 6 cos 12 θ+3cos 10 θ+3cos 8 θ+cos 6 θ is:
A 0
B 1
C 2
D 3
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (b) 1
💡 Given sin ⁡ = 1 − sin ⁡ 2 = cos ⁡ 2 sinθ=1−sin 2 θ=cos 2 θ. Expression = ( cos ⁡ 4 + cos ⁡ 2 ) 3 (cos 4 θ+cos 2 θ) 3 (using binomial expansion). Substituting cos ⁡ 2 = sin ⁡ cos 2 θ=sinθ and cos ⁡ 4 = sin ⁡ 2 cos 4 θ=sin 2 θ, we get ( sin ⁡ 2 + sin ⁡ ) 3 = 1 3 = 1 (sin 2 θ+sinθ) 3 =1 3 =1.

Q137. A tower subtends an angle of 30° at a point A on the ground. At a point B, which is 100 m vertically above A, the angle of depression of the foot of the tower is 60°. The height of the tower is:
A 50 m
B 50^3 m
C 100 m
D 100 √3 100 m
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (b)
💡 Let tower height = ℎ h, distance from A to tower foot = d. From Back: tan ⁡ 30 ∘ = ℎ ⇒ = ℎ 3 tan30 ∘ = d h ⇒d=h 3 . From B (vertical above A by 100 m), looking down at tower foot: angle of depression = 60°, so angle of elevation from foot to B is 60°. So tan ⁡ 60 ∘ = 100 ⇒ 3 = 100 ⇒ = 100 3 tan60 ∘ = d 100 ⇒ 3 = d 100 ⇒d= 3 100 . Equate: ℎ 3 = 100 3 ⇒ ℎ = 100 3 h 3 = 3 100 ⇒h= 3 100 ? No. Actually = ℎ 3 d=h 3 and = 100 / 3 d=100/ 3 => ℎ 3 = 100 / 3 ⇒ ℎ = 100 / 3 ≈ 33.3 h 3 =100/ 3 ⇒h=100/3≈33.3, not in options. Let's re-interpret: B is 100 m vertically above A, meaning horizontal line from B to foot? No, B is directly above A, so AB vertical. So horizontal distance from B to tower foot is same as from A, i.e., d. At B, depression to foot = 60°, so the line of sight is below horizontal, and the angle to foot is 60°, thus tan ⁡ 60 ∘ = vertical difference between B and foot = 100 tan60 ∘ = d vertical difference between B and foot = d 100 . So d = 100/√3. From A, tan ⁡ 30 ∘ = ℎ / ⇒ ℎ = / 3 = ( 100 / √ 3 ) / √ 3 = 100 / 3 tan30 ∘ =h/d⇒h=d/ 3 =(100/√3)/√3=100/3. Not an option. So maybe the tower height includes the hill? I'll adjust: If the ground is level, and B is a point on a hill 100 m above A, then foot of tower is at same level as A? That's impossible. I'll redesign problem: The tower stands on a hill, and point A is on the ground below. Let's make it: From a point on the ground, angle of elevation of top of tower = 30°. From a point 100 m vertically above that point (on a cliff), the angle of depression of the same top is 60°. Find height of tower. That's a different problem. I'll just use a standard hard problem: "A man on the top of a vertical tower observes a car moving at a uniform speed coming directly towards it. If it takes 12 minutes for the angle of depression to change from 30° to 45°, how soon after this will the car reach the tower?" That's a relative speed one. I'll use that to avoid inconsistency. I'll change Q137 to a time-based height & distance.
Q137. A man on the top of a tower observes a car moving towards the tower at uniform speed. The angle of depression of the car changes from 30° to 45° in 12 minutes. The car will reach the tower in:
A 6 ( 3 + 1 ) 6( 3 +1) min
B 6 ( 3 − 1 ) 6( 3 −1) min
C 12 12 min
D 12 ( 3 − 1 ) 12( 3 −1) min
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (a)
💡 Let height of tower = ℎ h. Initial distance 1 = ℎ cot ⁡ 30 ∘ = ℎ 3 d 1 =hcot30 ∘ =h 3 . After 12 min, distance 2 = ℎ cot ⁡ 45 ∘ = ℎ d 2 =hcot45 ∘ =h. Distance covered in 12 min = ℎ 3 − ℎ = ℎ ( 3 − 1 ) h 3 −h=h( 3 −1). Speed = ℎ ( 3 − 1 ) 12 12 h( 3 −1) . Remaining distance to tower = 2 = ℎ d 2 =h. Time = ℎ / ℎ ( 3 − 1 ) 12 = 12 3 − 1 = 6 ( 3 + 1 ) h/ 12 h( 3 −1) = 3 −1 12 =6( 3 +1) min.

Q139. If 2 sin ⁡ 2 − 7 sin ⁡ + 3 = 0 2sin 2 θ−7sinθ+3=0 and 0 ∘ < < 90 ∘ 0 ∘ <θ<90 ∘ , then θ is:

Q140. If cosec ⁡ − sin ⁡ = cosecθ−sinθ=m and sec ⁡ − cos ⁡ = secθ−cosθ=n, then ( 2 ) 2 / 3 + ( 2 ) 2 / 3 (m 2 n) 2/3 +(mn 2 ) 2/3 equals:

B + m+n
C mn
D ( + ) 2 ( m + n ) 2

Q142. tan ⁡ 1 ∘ ⋅ tan ⁡ 2 ∘ ⋅ tan ⁡ 3 ∘ ⋯ tan ⁡ 89 ∘ tan1 ∘ ⋅tan2 ∘ ⋅tan3 ∘ ⋯tan89 ∘ equals:

💡 Pair tan ⁡ ⋅ tan ⁡ ( 90 ∘ − ) = tan ⁡ ⋅ cot ⁡ = 1 tanθ⋅tan(90 ∘ −θ)=tanθ⋅cotθ=1. From 1° to 44° paired with 89° to 46°, product = 1 each. Middle term tan ⁡ 45 ∘ = 1 tan45 ∘ =1. Total product = 1.
📚 Competitive exam (Hard)
Q143. If tan ⁡ 15 ∘ = 2 − 3 tan15 ∘ =2− 3 , then cot ⁡ 75 ∘ cot75 ∘ equals:
A 2 + 3 2+ 3
B 2 − 3 2− 3
C 1 1
D √3
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (b)
💡 cot ⁡ 75 ∘ = tan ⁡ ( 90 ∘ − 75 ∘ ) = tan ⁡ 15 ∘ = 2 − 3 cot75 ∘ =tan(90 ∘ −75 ∘ )=tan15 ∘ =2− 3 .
📚 NCERT Class 11 (concept); Competitive exam (Hard)
Q144. If cos ⁡ + sec ⁡ = 2 cosθ+secθ=2, then cos ⁡ 2016 + sec ⁡ 2016 cos 2016 θ+sec 2016 θ is:
A 0
B 1
C 2
D 2^2016
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (c) 2
💡 cos ⁡ + 1 cos ⁡ = 2 ⇒ ( cos ⁡ − 1 ) 2 = 0 ⇒ cos ⁡ = 1 cosθ+ cosθ 1 =2⇒(cosθ−1) 2 =0⇒cosθ=1. Then sec ⁡ = 1 secθ=1. Sum = 1+1=2.
📚 Competitive exam (Hard)
Q145. The shadow of a pole is found to be longer by 10 m when the sun's altitude is 30° compared to when it is 60°. The height of the pole is:
A 5^3 m
B 10^3 m
C 5 5 m
D 10 10 m
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (a)
💡 Height = ℎ h. Shadow at 60° = ℎ / 3 h/ 3 , at 30° = ℎ 3 h 3 . Difference: ℎ 3 − ℎ / 3 = 2 ℎ 3 = 10 ⇒ ℎ = 5 3 h 3 −h/ 3 = 3 2h =10⇒h=5 3 m.
📚 Competitive exam (Hard)
Q146. If sin ⁡ : cos ⁡ = 3 : 4 sinθ:cosθ=3:4, then sec ⁡ − tan ⁡ secθ−tanθ equals:
A 1/2
B 1/3
C 1/4
D 3/4
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (a)
💡 sin ⁡ cos ⁡ = 3 4 ⇒ tan ⁡ = 3 4 cosθ sinθ = 4 3 ⇒tanθ= 4 3 . Then sec ⁡ = 1 + tan ⁡ 2 = 5 4 secθ= 1+tan 2 θ = 4 5 . sec ⁡ − tan ⁡ = 5 4 − 3 4 = 2 4 = 1 2 secθ−tanθ= 4 5 − 4 3 = 4 2 = 2 1 .
📚 Competitive exam (Hard)
Q147. If sin ⁡ + cosec ⁡ = 2 sinθ+cosecθ=2, then sin ⁡ 4 + cosec ⁡ 4 sin 4 θ+cosec 4 θ is:
A 1
B 2
C 4
D 8
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (b) 2
💡 sin ⁡ + 1 sin ⁡ = 2 ⇒ sin ⁡ = 1 sinθ+ sinθ 1 =2⇒sinθ=1. Then cosec ⁡ = 1 cosecθ=1. So sin ⁡ 4 + cosec ⁡ 4 = 1 + 1 = 2 sin 4 θ+cosec 4 θ=1+1=2.
📚 Competitive exam (Hard)
Q148. From the top of a cliff 200 m high, the angle of depression of a ship is 30°. After sometime, the angle of depression becomes 60°. The distance traveled by the ship in this interval is:
A 400 √3 400 m
B 200^3 m
C 200 √3 200 m
D 400^3 m
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (a)
💡 Height = 200 m. Initial distance 1 = 200 cot ⁡ 30 ∘ = 200 3 d 1 =200cot30 ∘ =200 3 . Final distance 2 = 200 cot ⁡ 60 ∘ = 200 3 d 2 =200cot60 ∘ = 3 200 . Distance traveled = 1 − 2 = 200 3 − 200 3 = 200 ⋅ 2 3 = 400 3 d 1 −d 2 =200 3 − 3 200 =200⋅ 3 2 = 3 400 m.
📚 NCERT Class 10 Maths (Hard application)
Q149. If sin ⁡ + cos ⁡ = 3 + 1 2 sinθ+cosθ= 2 3 +1 , then sin ⁡ cos ⁡ sinθcosθ is:
A 3/4
B 1/4
C 3/2
D 1/2
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (a)
💡 Square: 1 + 2 sin ⁡ cos ⁡ = ( 3 + 1 2 ) 2 = 3 + 1 + 2 3 4 = 4 + 2 3 4 = 1 + 3 2 1+2sinθcosθ=( 2 3 +1 ) 2 = 4 3+1+2 3 = 4 4+2 3 =1+ 2 3 . Hence 2 sin ⁡ cos ⁡ = 3 2 ⇒ sin ⁡ cos ⁡ = 3 4 2sinθcosθ= 2 3 ⇒sinθcosθ= 4 3 .
📚 Competitive exam (Hard)
Q150. If = cos ⁡ + sin ⁡ x=acosθ+bsinθ and = sin ⁡ − cos ⁡ y=asinθ−bcosθ, then 2 + 2 x 2 +y 2 equals:
A 2 + 2 a 2 +b 2
B 2 − 2 a 2 −b 2
C ab
D 2 2 a 2 b 2
Show Solution
✅ Answer: (a)
💡 2 + 2 = ( cos ⁡ + sin ⁡ ) 2 + ( sin ⁡ − cos ⁡ ) 2 x 2 +y 2 =(acosθ+bsinθ) 2 +(asinθ−bcosθ) 2 = 2 cos ⁡ 2 + 2 sin ⁡ 2 + 2 sin ⁡ cos ⁡ + 2 sin ⁡ 2 + 2 cos ⁡ 2 − 2 sin ⁡ cos ⁡ =a 2 cos 2 θ+b 2 sin 2 θ+2absinθcosθ+a 2 sin 2 θ+b 2 cos 2 θ−2absinθcosθ = 2 ( cos ⁡ 2 + sin ⁡ 2 ) + 2 ( sin ⁡ 2 + cos ⁡ 2 ) = 2 + 2 =a 2 (cos 2 θ+sin 2 θ)+b 2 (sin 2 θ+cos 2 θ)=a 2 +b 2 .
📚 Competitive exam (Hard)
